The detector acceptance multiplied by the efficiency (
A\cdot\epsilon
) is calculated for several signal regions and their
benchmark points. The
A\cdot\epsilon
values for signal regions aimed at high-energy final states, SRA and SRE, are 9% and 6% for their respective signal benchmark points of
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=1000{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=1{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
, and
m_{\tilde{g}}=1700{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=400{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=395{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
. SRB, SRD-low, and SRD-high have
A\cdot\epsilon
of 1.4%, 0.05%, and 0.5% for
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=600{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=300{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
;
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=400{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=100{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=50{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
; and
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=700{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=200{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=100{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
where the branching ratio,
B
(
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
) = 100% is assumed for the SRD samples, respectively. Finally, SRC1–5 (combining the
R_{\mathrm{ISR}}
windows) has an
A\cdot\epsilon
of 0.08% for
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=400{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}},m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=227{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
.

The profile-likelihood-ratio test statistic is used to set limits on direct pair production of top squarks. The signal strength parameter is allowed to float in the fit
[
84
]
, and any signal contamination in the CRs is taken into account. Again, limits are derived using the CL
s
prescription and calculated from asymptotic formulae. Orthogonal signal subregions, such as SRA-TT, SRA-TW, and SRA-T0, are statistically combined by multiplying their likelihood functions. A similar procedure is performed for the signal subregions in SRB and SRC. For the overlapping signal regions defined for SRD (SRD-low and SRD-high), the signal region with the smallest expected CL
s
value is chosen for each signal model. Once the signal subregions are combined or chosen, the signal region with the smallest expected CL
s
is chosen for each signal model in the
\tilde{t}_{1}
–
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
signal grid. The nominal event yield in each SR is set to the mean background expectation to determine the expected limits; contours that correspond to
\pm 1\sigma
uncertainties in the background estimates (
\sigma_{\mathrm{exp}}
) are also evaluated. The observed event yields determine the observed limits for each SR; these are evaluated for the nominal signal cross sections as well as for
\pm 1\sigma
theory uncertainties in those cross sections, denoted by
\sigma^{\mathrm{SUSY}}_{\mathrm{theory}}
.

Figure
8
shows the observed (solid red line) and
expected (solid blue line) exclusion contours at 95% CL in the
\tilde{t}_{1}
–
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
mass plane for
36.1\,\mbox{fb${}^{-1}$}
.
The data excludes top-squark masses between

450
and
1000
GeV for
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
masses below
160{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
, extending Run-1 limits from the combination of zero- and one-lepton channels by 260 GeV. Additional constraints are set in the case where
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}\approx m_{t}+m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}
, for which top-squark masses in the range
235\mathrm{-}590
GeV are excluded. The limits in this region of the exclusion are new compared to the 8 TeV results and come from the inclusion of SRC, which takes advantage of an ISR system to discriminate between signal and the dominant
t\bar{t}
background.

For signal models also considering top-squark decays into
b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
or into additional massive neutralinos, four interpretations are considered:

Natural SUSY-inspired mixed grid:

A simplified model
[
88
]
where
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+1{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
with only two decay modes,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
and
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
, and only on-shell top-quark decays are considered. The same
maximal mixing between the partners of the left- and right-handed top quarks
and nature of the
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
(pure bino) as for the
B
(
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
)=100% case is assumed. The branching ratio to
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
is set to 0%, 25%, 50%, and 75% and yield the limits shown in Figure
9
.

A pMSSM-inspired simplified model with a higgsino LSP,
{m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+5{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}}

,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}

[

]
. A set of branching ratios with

(

) = 33%, 33%, 33% is considered, which is equivalent to a pMSSM model with the lightest top squark mostly consisting of the superpartner of left-handed top quark and

(ratio of vacuum expectation values of the two Higgs doublets). Additionally,

(

,

(

Wino-NLSP pMSSM:

A pMSSM model where the LSP is bino-like and has mass
M_{1}
and where the NLSP is wino-like with mass
M_{2}
, while
M_{2}=2M_{1}
and
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}>M_{1}

[

]
. Limits are set for both positive and negative

(the higgsino mass parameter) as a function of the

and

masses which can be translated to different

and

. Only bottom and top-squark production are considered in this interpretation. Allowed decays in the top-squark production scenario are
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}\to h/Z\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
, at a maximum branching ratio of 33%, and
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}

dominantly decays into a

or

is determined by the sign of
\mu
. Along the diagonal region, the
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
decay with 100% branching ratio is also considered. The equivalent decays in bottom-squark production are
\tilde{b}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
and

. The remaining pMSSM parameters have the following values:
M_{3}=2.2
TeV (gluino mass parameter),
M_{S}=\sqrt{m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}m_{\tilde{t}_{2}}}=1.2
TeV (geometric mean of top-squark masses),
X_{t}/M_{S}=\sqrt{6}
(mixing parameter between the superpartners of left- and right-handed states, where

and

is the trilinear coupling parameter in the top-quark sector), and
\mathrm{tan}\beta=20
. All other pMSSM masses are set to
>
3 TeV.

and
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}
for the pMSSM-inspired non-asymptotic higgsino simplified model for a small tan
\beta
with
B
(
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
) = 45%, 10%, 45% (blue), a large tan
\beta
with
B
(
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
) = 33%, 33%, 33% (red), and a small
\tilde{t}_{R}
with
B
(
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
,
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
) = 25%, 50%, 25% (green) assumption. Uncertainty bands correspond to the
\pm 1\sigma
variation of the expected limit.

and

. Uncertainty bands correspond to the

variation of the expected limit.

and

\tilde{t}_{L}
scan (red) as well as for the
\tilde{t}_{R}
scan (blue) in the well-tempered pMSSM model. Uncertainty bands correspond to the
\pm 1\sigma
variation of the expected limit.

The SRE results are interpreted for indirect top-squark production
through gluino decays in terms of the
\tilde{t}_{1}
vs
\tilde{g}
mass
plane with
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=5{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
.
Gluino masses up to
m_{\tilde{g}}=1800{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
with
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}<800{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
are excluded as shown in Figure
13
.

\tilde{g}\to t\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}+
soft
and
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=5{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
. Uncertainty bands corresponding to the

and

TeV are overlaid in grey and blue

,

,

]
.

10
Conclusions

Results from a search for top squark pair production based
on an integrated luminosity of
36.1\,\mbox{fb${}^{-1}$}
of
\sqrt{s}=13{\mathrm{\ Te\kern-1.00006ptV}}\penalty\ pp

collision data recorded by the ATLAS experiment at the LHC in 2015 and
2016 are presented. Top squarks are searched for in
final states with high-
p_{\mathrm{T}}
jets and large missing transverse
momentum. In this paper, direct top squark production is studied assuming top squarks decay via
\tilde{t}_{1}\rightarrow t^{(*)}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
with large or small mass differences between the top squark and the neutralino
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
and via
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
, where
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+1{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
.
Additionally, gluino-mediated
\tilde{t}_{1}
production is
studied, in which gluinos decay via
\tilde{g}\rightarrow t\tilde{t}_{1}
, with a
small
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
.

No significant excess above the expected SM background is observed. Exclusion limits at 95% confidence level in the plane of the top-squark and LSP masses are derived, resulting in the exclusion of top-squark masses in the range

450\mathrm{-}1000
GeV for
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
masses below
160{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
. For the case where
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}\sim m_{t}+m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}
, top-squark masses in the range
235\mathrm{-}590
GeV are excluded. In addition, model-independent limits and
p
-values for each signal region are reported. Limits that take into account an additional decay of
\tilde{t}_{1}\to b\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}
are also set with an exclusion of top-squark masses between 450 and 850 GeV for
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}<240{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
and
B(\tilde{t}_{1}\to t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=50\%
for
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+1{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
. Limits are also derived in two pMSSM models, where one model assumes a wino-like NLSP and the other model is constrained by the dark-matter relic density. In addition to limits in pMSSM slices, limits are set in terms of one pMSSM-inspired simplified model where
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+5{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
and
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+10{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
. Finally, exclusion contours are reported for gluino production where
m_{\tilde{t}_{1}}=m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}+5{\mathrm{\ Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}}
, resulting in gluino masses being constrained to be above 1800 GeV for
\tilde{t}_{1}
masses below 800 GeV.
